When you visit more websites, each opens up a “tracker” or fingerprint—almost like a code that uniquely identifies you. Since visiting the same IP (where you physically are) usually means from the same house/appartment, most sites perceive it as strong evidence for distinguishing you from someone else’s device at that exact spot.**

I understand that you'd like to adjust the font size for your texts but aren't sure how. When dealing with responsive design, it's crucial to keep factors like the font ratio in mind. 

This is typically done through a CSS technique called breakpoints, or media queries. By setting different font sizes for different screen sizes, you can ensure that your design is adjustable and performant across a wide range of devices. 

More precisely, the general concept to follow includes:

1. Setting specific and observable breakpoints - check the width of the viewport, screens per breakpoint, viewport height, and minimum and default font sizes of devices of each breakpoint, plus other essential considerations.

2. Applying breakpoints based on ratio within the CSS code.

3. For irregular elements where breakpoints get trickier, or for tweening fluid images up or down, hover classes can often be added for accessible modification.

Remember, responsiveness is key.
